Changes

Every change, with its source

Corrections and additions to the record, and dated milestones on the ground.

Coming upRecentAll

All changes

Category
All categories
What do these categories mean?
StatusThe record’s status changed -- planned to under construction, say.
MilestoneA dated event on the ground.
ConstructionConstruction progress.
RetailA tenant or storefront opening.
Open spaceA park, plaza or paseo added to the record.
CorrectionSomething already on the record was fixed.
CoverageA new record, photo or piece of research added.
Coming upExpected, not yet happened.

  • 2042StatusPier 70

    Pier 70 Parcel A: Completion expected only by the Pier 70 28-Acre Project's whole-project buildout target, since Phase 1's own DDA schedule has lapsed and vertical construction on this parcel remains pausedexpected, by project buildout target: Phase 1's DDA outside date (about 2025, 7.5 years after the 2017 Project approval) has already passed with no vertical construction started, per the Port's August 2026 report on the ongoing pause; this instead uses the DDA's whole-project outside date for Phase 3, the Project's final phase, of approximately 25 years after approval (about 2042), from the Schedule of Performance in Port Commission Item 9B (Sept 22, 2017, Table A, p.7).

  • 2031StatusPotrero Station

    Humboldt Street Plaza: Completion expected: Humboldt Street Plaza is a Phase 2 open space under the revised Development Agreement phasing, keyed to a 3-million-sq-ft occupancy trigger rather than a calendar dateexpected, by phase schedule: Phase 2 in the October 2020 Phase 1 and Phasing Plan Amendment (revised from six phases to three over the same 16 years); SocketSite (Oct 9, 2020) reports Phase 2 running 2026-2031. The plaza's actual delivery trigger is the first certificate of occupancy for the building representing 3 million sq ft of total development, not a calendar date, so this is Phase 2's outer window, not a plaza-specific date.

    California Barrel Company / SF Planning (M-Files: 'Potrero Power Station Phase 1 and Phasing Plan Amendment Applications'), 2020-10; SocketSite, 2020-10-09
  • 2031StatusPotrero Station

    Waterfront Park: Completion expected: Waterfront Park South, North and West are Phase 2 under the revised Development Agreement phasing (The Point, the smaller Phase 1 piece, has no design review approval and no dated schedule of its own beyond its Dec 2021 submittal)expected, by phase schedule: Phase 2 in the October 2020 Phase 1 and Phasing Plan Amendment (revised from six phases to three over the same 16 years); SocketSite (Oct 9, 2020) reports Phase 2 running 2026-2031. This covers the bulk of the waterfront open space (South, North and West); The Point is nominally Phase 1 (tied to a 500,000-sq-ft occupancy trigger) but its design review has been pending since Dec 2021 with no schedule of its own, so the record uses the later, larger Phase 2 window rather than claim a Phase 1 date for the whole park.

    California Barrel Company / SF Planning (M-Files: 'Potrero Power Station Phase 1 and Phasing Plan Amendment Applications'), 2020-10; SocketSite, 2020-10-09

  • Sep 23, 2026CoverageAll sites

    Split Chase Center's shared parcel from Uber Mission Bay 3 & 4; merged Harmonic Brewing's building in.

    Full text

    The owner identified that DataSF's Assessor Parcel dataset gives Chase Center (formerly tracked as status.csv blklot 8722025) and the neighboring Uber Mission Bay 3 & 4 office towers (formerly blklot 8722063) byte-identical geometry -- one shared master-condominium-association land parcel spanning the whole Mission Bay South Blocks 29-32 superblock (confirmed: Chase Center, both Uber towers, a parking structure and a small broadcast building are all separate condo units on one shared plat). This made the two records' polygons fully overlapping on the map -- a click anywhere in the shared area could resolve to either record, and each record's own "Where it is" mini-map showed the wrong footprint. Migrated both records off status.csv onto hand-derived, non-overlapping real geometry: Chase Center (record_id mission_bay_chase_center) is now the full Assessor parcel with both Uber towers' own real OpenStreetMap building footprints subtracted out; Uber Mission Bay 3 & 4 (record_id mission_bay_uber_3_4) is now the union of those same two real OSM footprints. The owner also identified that Harmonic Brewing's own standalone taproom building, at the NE edge of the site (7 Warriors Way Ste 206, confirmed via its own DataSF business-registration address and geocoded location), and Miller & Lux (7 Warriors Way Ste 208, confirmed inside the arena's own footprint) are both Chase Center/Thrive City tenants, not separate structures -- Harmonic Brewing's building is included in Chase Center's new geometry (it falls within the parcel-minus-Uber-towers remainder) rather than tracked as a separate record. Re-running the retail spatial join (src/join_retail.py) against the corrected geometry surfaced three more real, previously-untracked Thrive City tenants -- Cupcakin' Bake Shop, Siren Cafe & Bar and The Sarap Shop -- added separately; see those changelog rows.

    Full text

    Verified a second Gemini research batch covering the 16 records this project had deliberately left undated (docs/briefs/gemini-verification-2.md). Confirmed and added: Mission Bay Block 34 gained a May 2020 UC Regents preliminary-plans item and its August 2024 completion date firmed to the exact day (Aug 27), while a claimed April 2021 garage-construction-start date was checked and rejected -- a Commercial Property Executive article directly contradicts it, showing the garage broke ground after, not before, the clinical building. Park P10 gained two real earlier-stage milestones from a directly-read EPA/DTSC brownfields fact sheet that names "Mission Bay Park P10" specifically, and a 2011 press-tour article -- while a companion Board of Supervisors citation Gemini gave for a different record (P11/P11A) turned out, on direct Legistar lookup, to reference an unrelated Graffiti Advisory Board appointment and was rejected outright. Avalon at Mission Bay II gained a real Q1 2005 construction-start date from the developer's own SEC-filed earnings release. 255 Berry's existing 2004 completion milestone had its source upgraded from a generic listing search to the developer's own project page. Mission Creek Park (the P3 extension) gained an October 2022 completion date from a Landscape Performance Series case study, closing a gap this record's own open_questions had already flagged (whether a fall-2021 target opening actually happened -- it slipped a year further). Two records (the P26 interchange wedge and Block 22) had Gemini's claimed dates confirmed as flat misattributions -- the cited Port Commission/Lotus Water material is entirely about the already-tracked Bayfront Park (P22), not either record -- so nothing was added to either. Five more records (The Beacon, Mission Bay Commons, the Creek Park's own P1-P3 segment as distinct from P3, and the three UCSF garage/utility-plant records) returned no independently confirmable date after this pass and were left as-is, several due to realestate.ucsf.edu's persistent block on automated access.

    Full text

    Verified a third Gemini research batch (docs/briefs/gemini-verification-3.md), following ground rules the project gave it after two earlier rounds. Citations were real and quotable far more often this time, but two of the 13 claims were still rejected before verification even started -- both cited real documents describing a different physical parcel than this project tracks (Block 22 vs. Bayfront Park/P22, confirmed ~500m apart and matching UCSF's own "Koret Quad" label; the P26 interchange wedge vs. the real, separately-named Mariposa Park, confirmed via San Francisco's own Legistar API that the cited ordinance's own title places it on a different street). Of the remaining 11: confirmed and added -- Uber Mission Bay 3 & 4 gained two 2018 structural-steel milestones (Mortenson's own steel-fabrication page, distinct from the arena's steel package) and a JV-formation note (Alexandria Real Estate's own SEC filing); Mission Bay Commons gained a narrower, accurately-worded Nov 2011 completion milestone for Park P16 specifically (the source names only P16, not P17, and says "recently completed," not "dedicated," as Gemini's fuller claim implied); Mission Bay Block 34 gained a real April 2021 garage-construction-start date from Clark Construction's own press release, reversing this project's own prior rejection of the same date (which had relied on a vaguer secondary paraphrase). Caught and rejected: P11/P11A's claimed Board of Supervisors File #190755 was re-checked directly against Legistar's API and confirmed, again, not to exist; UCSF's 1670 Owens Garage citation turned out to be about a different, separately-addressed UCSF garage (1625 Owens St, serving the Rutter Center) three streets away. Flagged, not resolved: a real identity question about whether mission_bay_creek_park_p1_p3 actually belongs to Mission Bay North's own NP1/NP2 open-space system rather than the Mission Bay South labels it currently carries. Left as-is: the UCSF Preliminary Utilities Plant and part of the Third Street Garage's own completion date, both blocked by realestate.ucsf.edu's persistent 403 (tried direct fetch, a reader-proxy, spoofed-user-agent curl, and the Wayback Machine -- the Wayback Machine itself is blocked at this environment's network level). The Third Street Garage did gain a general-contractor fact (Overaa Construction) confirming its identity and surfacing an 822-vs-882-space discrepancy between a 2003 pre-construction budget and the contractor's own as-built figure.

    Second correction pass: a real-block coverage audit found 9 under-tracked blocks, fixed a geometry bug and added two new real records.

    Full text

    SECOND correction pass on Mission Bay coverage claims, this time using a proper method: rather than eyeballing footprints, polygonized real city blocks from real street geometry (src/block_fit.py's load_blocks(), the same tool used elsewhere in this project to fit new features against real blocks) and computed, for every real block touching the Mission Bay boundary, what fraction of its actual building footprint area is covered by a tracked record. Of 58 real blocks with >=30% of their area inside the boundary, 33 have meaningful real building coverage (>3% of block area built); this audit found 9 of those 33 were under 50% tracked. Investigated all 9: fixed a real bug in 500 Terry A. Francois Boulevard's own geometry (mission_bay_500_terry_francois.geojson) -- it had only ever captured 1 of 3 real DataSF footprint pieces sharing its mblr (the smallest, ~1172 sq m, missing ~7460 sq m of the real ~8636 sq m building), now corrected to the union of all 3; added 1401 Third Street (~222,000 sq ft office building, built 2009, tenant not identified) and the Chase Center Parking Garage (150 Warriors Way, 469,726 sq ft structure, built 2009) as new real records. Four blocks remain under 50% tracked: all are minor (building footprint under 17% of their block's area) with no address found in DataSF Assessor parcel data to identify them -- left undocumented rather than guessed, same treatment as the already-investigated 'Community Center Garage' fragment near the Rutter Community Center. This audit method (real polygonized blocks + building-footprint coverage fraction) is more rigorous than the ad hoc satellite spot-checks and footprint-distance scans used in the prior two passes and should be the standard going forward for any future Mission Bay completeness claim.

    Mission Rock map, round 2: fixed a false pinch in the rendered street edge near Block H caused by a partial curb segment.

    Full text

    Mission Rock labeled map, round 2: the round-1 street-corridor fix above unioned in a real DataSF curb LineString that turns out to cover only part of Block H's Mission Rock St frontage, pulling the rendered street edge in tight under part of H (~1.2m gap) while the rest of the D1/D2/H frontage kept the generic ~3.9-4.3m gap -- a false pinch/step the owner flagged as looking wrong ('should be a straight line'). Added an include_curbs flag to street_geometry.build_street_buffer() (default True, unchanged for every clipping/QA caller) and set it False only for this one map's rendering, so a partial curb segment can't make an already-straight real street look broken on the one map built to show it plainly. No geometry or clipping logic changed.

    UCSF garages pass: real OSM building footprints added for two of UCSF's Mission Bay parking structures (Third Street Garage, 1670 Owens Garage). A third named garage, Owens Street Garage, was investigated and found to genuinely overlap the already-committed UCSF hospital polygon by ~2,156 sq m on check_geometry_quality.py -- left OUT rather than added with an unresolved conflict; needs a manual satellite/site-plan check before a future pass can add it. A fourth candidate, 450 South St, was confirmed to be a private Alexandria Real Estate garage serving its own office campus, not a UCSF building at all, and was not added under the UCSF pass.

    docs/briefs/mission-bay-geometry.md
  • Sep 18, 2026CoverageAll sites

    Mission Bay North pass: 5 real footprints added for Berry St residential buildings, plus the Mission Bay Branch Library.

    Full text

    Mission Bay North pass: 5 real OSM building footprints added for condo-mapped residential buildings along Berry St not previously in the dataset -- Arterra (300 Berry St, ~275 units, 2009), Park Terrace (325 Berry St, 110 units, 2007), Edgewater (355 Berry St; developer/year/unit-count not confirmed by any source found, left as an open question), 235 Berry (99 units, 2007) and 255 Berry (2004) -- the latter two are Signature Properties' two Mission Bay buildings, informally 'Signature I/II' in some secondary sources but named here by address since neither carries that name in its own marketing. Also added Mission Bay Branch Library (see above) as the pass's one real-parcel civic addition. Point-in-polygon checked against the official Mission Bay North boundary by a research pass; several other candidates it considered (500/550 Terry Francois Blvd, China Basin Landing, 185 Channel St) were found to be South, contested, or unconfirmed and were not added.

    docs/briefs/mission-bay-geometry.md

    Full text

    Park P-number pass: added a p_number property (sourced, confirmed vs. inferred) to Mission Bay South's five existing real park records, tying them to OCII's own D4D/Infrastructure Plan open-space numbering (Bayfront Park = P22, confirmed; Mission Bay Commons = the OCII-named P11/P12/P13/P15/P16/P17 assemblage, exact sub-parcel unconfirmed; Mission Creek Park likely P1/P3; Mission Bay Commons interim west likely P12/P13/P15; Kids' Park likely P6). Did not split or redraw any real, community-surveyed OSM boundary to match individual P-parcels: no source found gives P-parcel-level geometry, and OSM's own mapped boundaries reflect what is actually built regardless of the underlying plan numbering.   • Sep 17, 2026CorrectionAll sites

    Mission Rock's eight unbuilt blocks and the Square re-traced from the Design Controls' Figure 6.2, fixing a mis-fitted earlier transform.

    Full text

  • Nov 13, 2002StatusMission Bay

    Hearst Tower: UC Regents approve a $112,816,000 budget and external financing for the Mission Bay Block 20 Housing and Retail Project -- one 15-story tower (this building) and three mid-rise buildings (North, West and South/Student Housing) around a shared courtyard, 298,800 residential asf and 430 apartment units total -- with construction to begin June 2003 and full occupancy anticipated by October 2005

    The Regents of the University of California, 2002-11
  • Nov 13, 2002StatusMission Bay

    The Regents of the University of California, 2002-11

  • 2001MilestonePier 70

    Pier 70 Building 113/114/115/116 (Union Iron Works Machine Shop): Machine shop closed by the Port and red-tagged for its hazardous unreinforced-masonry condition, after use for ship repair until then

    National Park Service / California Office of Historic Preservation (nomination prepared for the Port of San Francisco by Carey & Co.), 2014-04-17
  • ~2000MilestoneMission Rock

    Pier 48: Destroyed sections reconstructed in steel framing and plywood (1999-2000)

    SF Planning (EIR appendix), 2016-04-11
  • Oct 30, 2000StatusMission Bay

  • Oct 23, 2000StatusMission Bay

    Avalon at Mission Bay I: Groundbreaking for AvalonBay's 16-story apartment tower on the King/3rd/Berry/4th block (became Avalon at Mission Bay I, 255 King St), one of three buildings breaking ground together that day

    sfgate.com
  • May 2000StatusMission Bay

    Arthur and Toni Rembe Rock Hall: UC Regents Committee on Grounds and Buildings certifies a CEQA EIR addendum and approves the design of the Mission Bay Developmental Biology and Genetics Building (Building 19B) -- believed to be this building based on matching architect (Cesar Pelli and Associates with Flad Associates as executive architect), size (~171,000 gsf) and programs (human genetics, developmental neuroscience, developmental biology), though the Regents item does not use the 'Rock Hall' name; an $88,000,000 total project budget was approved in March 2000, with construction slated for 2001-2003

    The Regents of the University of California, 2000-05
  • May 2000StatusMission Bay

    William J. Rutter Community Center: UC Regents Committee on Grounds and Buildings certifies a CEQA EIR addendum and approves the design of the Mission Bay Campus Community Center Building (21B) -- this building, confirmed by matching architects (MBT Architecture as executive architect with Legorreta Arquitectos as design subconsultant) and program (fitness/recreation center, conference center, food service, retail); a $58,000,000 total project budget was approved in March 2000, with construction slated for 2001-2002

    The Regents of the University of California, 2000-05
  • Apr 18, 2000StatusMission Bay

    Rich Sorro Commons: SF Dept. of Building Inspection permit #200004187592 filed to erect a 5-story, 100-unit residential building at 150 Berry St

    DataSF / SF Department of Building Inspection
  • Mar 1999StatusMission Bay

    Genentech Hall: UC Regents approve an amended $222.4 million capital-improvement budget, funding plan, environmental documentation and design for the 'UC Hall Seismic Replacement, Mission Bay' project (Building 24) -- believed to be this building based on matching gross square footage (433,828 gsf) and programs (structural/chemical biology, molecular/cell/developmental biology), per a January 2000 Regents item recounting the March 1999 action; site preparation began October 1999 and steel-frame construction began December 1999, per the same January 2000 item

    The Regents of the University of California, 2000-01

    Hoodline, 2025-10
  • ~1997MilestonePier 70

    Crane Cove Park: Park first envisioned in the Port's Waterfront Land Use Plan; later carried in the 2010 Pier 70 Preferred Master Plan and 2012 Blue Greenway Planning and Design Guidelines

    Port of San Francisco, 2018-09-07
  • ~1996MilestoneMission Rock

    Pier 48: Fire destroys Shed C and the eastern third of Sheds A and B

    SF Planning (EIR appendix), 2016-04-11
  • ~1979StatusMission Bay

    Channel Pump Station: Channel Pump Station built (year already documented in this record's own status.csv narrative, citing DataSF Assessor parcel data and SF Chronicle coverage); upgraded 2010

    DataSF Assessor parcel record; SF Chronicle "A Mission Bay pump station delivers chic design"
  • 1965MilestonePotrero Station

    The Stack (Unit 3 smokestack): Unit 3 Power Block and the Boiler Stack constructed (both), extending the Third Street Industrial District's period of significance to 1965 as contributing resources

    SF Planning Department, 2018-10
  • 1965StatusPotrero Station

    The Stack (Unit 3 smokestack): The Boiler Stack (and the adjoining Unit 3 Power Block) are constructed, extending the Third Street Industrial District's period of significance to 1965; the Stack is retained today as an 'Existing Structure' under the Design for Development rather than replaced

    SF Planning Department, 2018-10
  • ~1945MilestonePier 70

    Pier 70 Building 21 (Substation No. 5, relocated): Identified on Bethlehem Steel's 1945 plan as Sub-Station No. 5 and Electric Shop No. 2, a government-owned building (owner before 1941: Columbia Steel Co.)

    National Park Service / California Office of Historic Preservation (nomination prepared for the Port of San Francisco by Carey & Co.), 2014-04-17
  • 1941StatusPier 70

    Historic Building 12: Built by U.S. Navy personnel as Plate Shop No. 2, the central building of the New Yard on ground leveled from Irish Hill

    National Park Service / California Office of Historic Preservation (nomination prepared for the Port of San Francisco by Carey & Co.), 2014-04-17
  • 1941StatusPier 70

    Pier 70 Building 2 (Warehouse No. 2): Built during World War II as Warehouse No. 2 to support hull construction at the Building 12 complex; sixth floor with drafting room added 1944

    National Park Service / California Office of Historic Preservation (nomination prepared for the Port of San Francisco by Carey & Co.), 2014-04-17
  • ~1941MilestonePier 70

    Pier 70 Building 104 (UIW Headquarters): Rear (north) elevation infilled and the building converted to the Navy Office and hospital, giving it its present rectangular footprint

    National Park Service / California Office of Historic Preservation (nomination prepared for the Port of San Francisco by Carey & Co.), 2014-04-17
  • ~1941StatusPier 70

    Pier 70 Building 14: Built as the Heavy Warehouse for equipment used in Machine Shop 1 and ship outfitting (NRHP date; the Port's documents say 1944)

    National Park Service / California Office of Historic Preservation (nomination prepared for the Port of San Francisco by Carey & Co.), 2014-04-17; Port of San Francisco, 2019-01-04
  • ~1938MilestoneMission Rock

    Pier 48: Outer end decked over and connecting Shed C built between Sheds A and B (1937-38)

    SF Planning (EIR appendix), 2016-04-11
  • ~1930StatusMission Rock

    Pier 48: Transit Sheds A and B completed (1929-30) by L.M. King; substructure built 1928-29

    SF Planning (EIR appendix), 2016-04-11
  • Jul 17, 1929MilestoneMission Rock

    Pier 48: Concrete bulkhead wharf completed by Healy-Tibbitts Construction Company

    SF Planning (EIR appendix), 2016-04-11
  • Apr 1926MilestoneMission Rock

    Pier 48: Plans for Pier 48 completed under Chief Engineer Frank G. White of the Board of State Harbor Commissioners

    SF Planning (EIR appendix), 2016-04-11
  • 1917StatusPier 70

    Pier 70 Building 101 (Bethlehem Steel HQ): Built as the Union Iron Works' new main office building to Frederick H. Meyer's design, reported at a cost of $250,000 and 350 staff

    National Park Service / California Office of Historic Preservation (nomination prepared for the Port of San Francisco by Carey & Co.), 2014-04-17
  • Jan 1916MilestonePier 70

    Pier 70 Building 113/114/115/116 (Union Iron Works Machine Shop): Contract of almost $100,000 awarded for the new one-story reinforced concrete foundry (Building 115), under construction by June 1916; Bethlehem's plans date 115/116 to 1917

    National Park Service / California Office of Historic Preservation (nomination prepared for the Port of San Francisco by Carey & Co.), 2014-04-17
  • 1914MilestonePier 70

    Pier 70 Building 113/114/115/116 (Union Iron Works Machine Shop): The two brick buildings joined by a reinforced concrete and steel connector

    National Park Service / California Office of Historic Preservation (nomination prepared for the Port of San Francisco by Carey & Co.), 2014-04-17
  • 1912StatusPier 70

    Pier 70 Building 102 (former Power House): Built as the Union Iron Works powerhouse to Charles Peter Weeks's design; equipment dating from 1913-1914 remained in 2013

    National Park Service / California Office of Historic Preservation (nomination prepared for the Port of San Francisco by Carey & Co.), 2014-04-17
  • 1901MilestonePotrero Station

    Potrero Power Station Block 15 / Station A: Station A construction begins (completed 1902, with a 1930-31 addition); individually eligible for the California Register under Criterion 1, and a contributor to the Third Street Industrial District

    SF Planning Department, 2018-10
  • 1901StatusPotrero Station

    Potrero Power Station Block 15 / Station A: Station A's original construction begins (completed 1902, with a 1930-31 addition), predating the Potrero Power Station redevelopment plan by more than a century; the building is retained today (rather than demolished) for adaptive reuse as an office building

    SF Planning Department, 2018-10
  • ~1900StatusPier 70

    Pier 70 Building 21 (Substation No. 5, relocated): Built circa 1900 as part of the Risdon Iron and Locomotive Works (shown as a machine shop and transformer house on the 1914 and 1936 Sanborn maps)

    National Park Service / California Office of Historic Preservation (nomination prepared for the Port of San Francisco by Carey & Co.), 2014-04-17
  • 1896StatusPier 70

    Pier 70 Building 104 (UIW Headquarters): Built as the Union Iron Works office building, designed by George Percy and Frederick Hamilton

    National Park Service / California Office of Historic Preservation (nomination prepared for the Port of San Francisco by Carey & Co.), 2014-04-17
  • 1885StatusPier 70

    Pier 70 Building 113/114/115/116 (Union Iron Works Machine Shop): Eastern portion of Building 113/114 (blacksmith and boiler shop) completed to civil engineer D. E. Melliss's design; western portion (machine shop) completed 1886

    National Park Service / California Office of Historic Preservation (nomination prepared for the Port of San Francisco by Carey & Co.), 2014-04-17
